CHAPTER – FOUR

BHAGAVAN’S DEVOTEES – THE COWS

1. GOMATHA LAKSHMI
The most favoured of all animal devotees of Bhagavan is

‘Cow-Lakshmi’. She was almost human in nature and behaviour.
She was extraordinarily devoted to Bhagavan and had totally
surrendered to him. The Grace and kindness he showered on
Lakshmi was exceptional. It is common belief that only humans
can attain Moksha. But in June 1948, Lakshmi the cow attained
liberation in the hands of Bhagavan – her Guru and Lord.
Bhagavan and devotees acknowledged her abiding devotion even
in her own lifetime and she was loved by one and all.

Lakshmi came to the Ashram only to be at the feet of
her Guru and seek complete release through his Grace.
Gentle, charming and lovable, Lakshmi was a personality that
one cannot help but adore, and whose life story is both
entertaining and inspiring.

Today there are about 125 cows and bulls in the Ashram.
Hundreds of devotees and visitors who come to
Ramanasramam are fed everyday with milk, curds and ghee.
From the cow dung biogas is generated for cooking. Cow
dung is also used as fertilizer for the Ashram plants. This is all
part of Gomatha Lakshmi’s legacy.

Here is the story of ‘Gomatha Lakshmi’ taken from the
reminiscences of some of Bhagavan’s devotees.



32 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Kumaramangalam is a small village near Gudiyatham.
Here lived a devotee of Bhagavan known as Arunachala Pillai.
He had a dream in which he was told to present Bhagavan
with a calf and a cow. He took this as a divine order.
Arunachala Pillai’s cow eventually became pregnant and gave
birth to a female calf.

On a Friday, in December 1926, six months after the
delivery of the calf, Arunachala Pillai made his way to
Tiruvannamalai bringing with him the cow and the calf. He
offered them with devotion to Bhagavan as a token of his
love. Bhagavan tried to dissuade Pillai politely by telling him
that there were neither proper facilities nor the resources in
the Ashram to take care of a cow and a calf. Furthermore, at
that time the jungle around the Ashram was extremely dense
with wild animals such as leopards and cheetahs roaming
about. For safety reasons the ashramites were reluctant to
keep a cow and a calf on the premises. But Arunachala Pillai
refused to take them back.

Bhagavan told him: “You have done your duty in presenting
the cow and calf to the Ashram. But who will be able to look
after them? Please keep them on our behalf and tend them.”

The owner of the cow and the calf was very adamant.
He replied, “I have made this humble gift and I will not
take them away, even if you cut my throat! ” 1

Seeing his insistence and the devotion behind it,
Bhagavan’s devotee Ramanatha Dikshitar declared
energetically: “I will look after the cow and the calf.” Dikshitar
(who passed away a few years earlier to Bhagavan, after some
forty years of Bhagavan’s gracious company), was then living



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 33

in the Ashram. He was a frail, dwarfish, insignificant looking
man from whom one would normally never expect any
vehemence. But on this occasion he seemed as if he was inspired
and he cried out, smiting his chest: “Here I am! I make myself
responsible for the upkeep of these animals.” So it was that,
owing to the insistent devotion of Arunachala Pillai and the
unusual vehemence of Ramanatha Dikshitar, the cow and her
calf came to live at the Ashram.2

Ramanatha Dikshitar tended the cow and the calf with
loving care and great kindness for three months. The calf
was very playful and had extraordinary devotion and love
for Bhagavan. After a while Ramanatha Dikshitar was unable
to look after these animals and they were handed over to a
devotee called Pashupathi Aiyar in Tiruvannamalai town. He
was a Kannadiga from Karnataka, a keeper of cattle and ran
a dairy business. The cow and calf were well looked after by
him. Thus a year passed.

It was a day of the eclipse. Pashupathi Aiyar had brought
the cow and the calf for a bath in Palithirtham, the tank next
to the Ashram. After the bath, Pashupathi Aiyar brought them
to the Ashram for Bhagavan’s darshan. The calf started to jump
with joy when she saw Bhagavan in the Ashram. She went
round him, and placed her forehead on Bhagavan’s feet. The
calf went around the Ashram and then Pashupathi Aiyar took
the cow and the calf back to his house in the town.

The calf seemed to have memorised the way to the
Ashram from her house in the town and also the layout of
the Ashram, because the next morning she came again all by
herself and stood in front of Bhagavan. Such was her



34 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

attraction to Bhagavan! From that day onwards, she came
alone from the town every morning, spent the day at the
Ashram and in the evening found her way back to Pashupathi
Aiyar’s house in the town. While at the Ashram, her attraction
to Bhagavan was so strong that she would hardly leave his
presence. Bhagavan on his part treated her with love and
care. The calf would lie down by the side of Bhagavan’s couch.
If food were available she would insist that Bhagavan alone
feed her. She particularly loved hill plantains. Every evening
before leaving for the town, to her house, she would go round
the hall and then depart reluctantly. In this way a year of
continuous and joyful satsang with Bhagavan passed, during
which time she came to be affectionately called as Lakshmi
(since she came to the Ashram on a Friday).3

By 1930, Lakshmi had already given birth to two male
calves. According to the understanding with Pashupathi
Aiyar, the male calves born to Lakshmi were to be given to
the Ashram. If Lakshmi had given birth to a female calf,
Pashupathi Aiyar would keep the calf himself. Lakshmi
became pregnant for the third time. One evening, she was
unwilling to leave Bhagavan and the Ashram to go back to
the town. She was lying close to Bhagavan’s couch and
shedding tears. Bhagavan was visibly affected. He softly passed
his hands over her face, and said, “What! You say you cannot
go away. You want to stay here only? What am I to do?” Looking
at others he said, “Look Lakshmi is weeping, saying she cannot
go away. She is pregnant and may deliver any moment. She has
to go a long way and come here again in the morning. She
cannot refrain from coming here. What is she to do?” 4



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 35

At last, Bhagavan somehow coaxed Lakshmi and sent her
home. That very night she delivered the calf. At about the same
time, Pashupathi Aiyar had some domestic problems. Unable
to bear Lakshmi with her vagaries, he brought her and her calf
and presented them to Bhagavan. Lakshmi sat at Bhagavan’s
feet and would not rise. Placing his right hand on her head and
pressing it, Bhagavan asked Lakshmi if she would like to stay in
the Ashram permanently. She closed her eyes and lay as if she
were in a trance. Noticing that, Bhagavan pointed out to the
others that it appeared as if she felt that her responsibility for
her calf was over because it had been placed in Bhagavan’s
charge. Thus Lakshmi and her calf came to stay permanently in
the Ashram from the year 1930. Lakshmi became one of the
favoured and prominent members of the Ashram.5

Shantammal, a devotee, who did the cooking in the
Ashram kitchen, recalls an incident involving Lakshmi:

“Lakshmi was pregnant for the third time. She came
into the hall after lunch. Bhagavan was reading a
newspaper. At that time, I also entered the hall. Lakshmi
went near Bhagavan and started licking the newspaper.

“Bhagavan looked up and said, ‘Wait a little,
Lakshmi!’ but she went on licking the paper.

“Bhagavan laid the newspaper aside put his hand
behind Lakshmi’s horns and rested his head against hers.
They stayed in this manner for quite a long time. I stood
nearby transfixed looking at the wonderful scene.

“After about ten minutes, Bhagavan without moving,
looked at me and said, ‘Do you know what Lakshmi is
doing? She is in samadhi.’



36 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

“I looked at Lakshmi and noticed that tears were
flowing in streams down her broad cheeks. Her breathing
had slowed down and her eyes were fixed on Bhagavan.

“After some time, Bhagavan changed his position and
asked gently, ‘Lakshmi, how do you feel now?’

“Lakshmi moved backwards, as if she was reluctant to
show her tail to Bhagavan. She walked round the hall and
went out.

“I felt elated and blessed to be there at that time to
witness the union of master and devotee.” 6

Bhagavan has told of a number of incidents in the
life of Lakshmi that showed that she had human
intelligence. He used to say that although she could not
speak the human language, she understood everything.
She used to come up to Bhagavan in the hall at mealtime,
and accompany him to the dining hall. In those days, there
was no mealtime bell. But Lakshmi was so punctual, that
even if Bhagavan was engaged in any other work and had
forgotten the time, he would turn and look at the clock
when Lakshmi came in. He would know it was mealtime.
She would go straight up to Bhagavan, and remind
Bhagavan that it was time for lunch. Her devotion gave
her a special right to go close to Bhagavan, and she took
no notice of the Ashram inmates or visitors who would be
present in the hall.7

Lakshmi’s special status in the Ashram gave her the
freedom to help herself to any food that was brought by
the devotees, because Bhagavan always supported Lakshmi
if devotees complained against her. He would also take her



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 37

side if anyone tried to prevent her from coming to see him
in the hall.

Bhagavan’s  devotee  N.N. Rajan narrates  another
anecdote  about  Lakshmi :

 “Once I was bringing foodstuff in a large open
vessel to offer to Bhagavan. Lakshmi, who had come
behind me, was eating from the vessel unnoticed by me.

“Bhagavan noticed it and remarked, ‘Enough
Lakshmi, enough! Leave something for us.’ So saying, he
gave some more foodstuffs to Lakshmi and then sent her
away. The attendant ridiculed and chided me for being
careless.

“But Bhagavan, out of Grace, said, ‘Why do you
blame him? Poor man, he is too innocent to notice all this.’

“Once ‘the certificate of innocence’ was received
from my Master, I said to myself, ‘what more do I require
in this life?’”8

Although, the cow Lakshmi had the entire pasture of
the Ashram to herself, she certainly did not content herself
with that. She knew when it was meal time and time for
tiffin. On both occasions she would walk right into the hall
and place her head on Bhagavan’s shoulder. Bhagavan would
stroke her with affection and call out to the people in the
kitchen to give her some food.

At that time in the Ashram, although there was water
shortage, a kitchen garden was grown with some difficulty.
As Lakshmi was young and frisky, she would occasionally
break in and play havoc with the young plants in the kitchen
garden and eat the fruits and vegetables. When those in



38 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

charge of the kitchen garden complained to Bhagavan about
what Lakshmi did. Bhagavan however, always took Lakshmi’s
side and defended her by saying, “She is not to be blamed.
She went where she found good food. If you don’t want her to go
there, you ought to have fenced the garden properly to keep her
out.” Lakshmi would stand there innocently like a child, who
had done mischief unknowingly.9

Lakshmi would walk off on her own accord from her
cowshed to the hall every morning and kneel at Bhagavan’s
feet. Then she would go to her shed to be milked. One
full-moon-day, the cowherd came earlier than usual. But
Lakshmi was not there in her shed. She had gone to
prostrate before Bhagavan. She did not find him in his usual
place. When she looked for him, she found Bhagavan was
in the kitchen cutting vegetables. Lakshmi called Bhagavan
to come out, and when he did, she bowed her head to touch
his feet. At that moment, the cowherd also came near the
kitchen looking for Lakshmi. The cowherd who witnessed
Lakshmi’s prostration to Bhagavan was dumb-founded with
awe and admiration. After prostrating before Bhagavan,
Lakshmi was now ready to go with the cowherd. The
cowherd appreciated Lakshmi’s devotion and the
relationship between the Guru and the devotee. The
photograph of Lakshmi prostrating before Bhagavan is still
there in the Ashram, and it is a popular photograph among
Bhagavan’s devotees.10

On one summer, there was a shortage of grass in the
Ashram, and Lakshmi was not getting enough of it. She
lost weight and had become very thin. Bhagavan noticed



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 39

this, and he was concerned that she was not getting enough
food. The next time he went to the dining room, he
refused to take food; instead he wanted to give it to
Lakshmi. The news of Bhagavan’s gesture reached
everybody. The workers realised that Bhagavan was
indirectly protesting against Lakshmi being underfed.
Some fodder was brought from the market, enabling both
Bhagavan and Lakshmi to resume their normal eating.
Bhagavan said, “Poor Lakshmi! She is a dumb animal; she
cannot speak any human language. Even if we only half feed
the animals they shall remain quiet. Why half starve them?
Are we half starving ourselves because it is summer?”11

The care and solicitude Bhagavan had for animals was
of the highest order.

Pascaline Mallet in her book Turn Eastwards: Glimpses
into Indian Life talks about Lakshmi the cow:

“Maharshi’s favourite cow, Lakshmi, is a most
beautiful creature, with delicate horns and almond shaped
eyes. The love she showed for her master was very touching.
As we were sitting in the Ashram, we heard a noise outside,
and to our amazement up the steps came Lakshmi, as if it
were the most natural thing in the world for a cow to
enter a room, and went straight up to Maharshi’s couch.
He at once greeted her affectionately and ordered that
she should be given some plantain to eat. Lakshmi then
quite satisfied turned round and began to make her way
through the crowd, heedless of the people seated on the
floor; she was eventually guided out amidst general
laughter.”12



40 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Lakshmi had nine calves, which added to the cattle
population of the Ashram. It has been widely reported that
Lakshmi often gave birth to her calf on Bhagavan’s Jayanthi
day. After delivering a new calf Lakshmi would walk into the
hall from her shed and stand mutely before Bhagavan. He
would then tell “Lakshmi, you have come to tell me that you
have a new baby. I will come to your shed to see the newborn.”
And he would accompany Lakshmi to her shed, see the new
calf and fondle it. The Ashram photographer Dr. T. N.
Krishnaswamy took a photograph of Bhagavan fondling the
calf, beaming with joy. This photograph can be seen in the
Ashram even today.13

The following incident was narrated by Sundaram Anna
(V.S.Ramanan):

“On another Jayanthi day of Bhagavan, the cow
Lakshmi came and stood near Bhagavan. He gave her
plantains as Prasad, but she refused to eat them, instead
she put her mouth near Bhagavan’s ear as if she was telling
him something. He immediately understood what she was
saying. She wanted iddlies first and then plantains.
Therefore she had refused to eat plantains as Bhagavan
had given fruits first.

“Bhagavan called his attendants and told them to
get iddlies for Lakshmi from the kitchen. When the
attendants went to the kitchen to get iddlies, they were
told that there were none, as there had been more
devotees than expected for breakfast that morning.
For the last batch uppuma had been prepared and
served.



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 41

“When the attendants came and reported to Bhagavan
that there were no iddlies left, Bhagavan remembered that
Mouni Sreenivas Rao, who worked in the Ashram office
till late at night, usually would get up a little late in the
morning. Therefore the kitchen people would put his
share of four iddlies in a lunch box and keep it on the top
of the cupboard in his room.

“Bhagavan knew of this and asked his attendants to
get one iddly from Mouni Sreenivas Rao’s room to give to
Lakshmi. When the iddly was brought, Lakshmi was fed
with that and then with plantains. After getting prasadam
from Bhagavan, Lakshmi went off happily to her shed.
Our Bhagavan who knew the likes and dislikes of his
devotees very well, fulfilled them like a Kalpatharu the
celestial wish-fulfilling tree.”14

One morning in January 1947, at about 9 am Lakshmi
entered the hall hurriedly with her legs, body and tail full of
mud, with blood oozing out of her nose and with a half-severed
rope round her neck. She went straight to the sofa where
Bhagavan sat. The attendants began saying with some disgust
that she had come in with mud on her body. Bhagavan,
however, said with affection, “Let her come. Let her come. What
does it matter how she comes?” Addressing the cow, he said. “Come,
my dear. Please come near.” So saying he passed his hand over the
body lightly, patted her on the neck, and looking at the face
said. “What is this? Some blood is oozing!” One of the attendants
said, “Recently they had put a rope through her nose”.

“Oho! Is that the reason? That is why she has come here to
complain to me about it. Is it not very painful for her? Unable



42 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

to bear the pain, she has come here running to complain to me
without even washing her body. What to do? Give her some
iddly or something,” said Bhagavan, evincing great solicitude
for her welfare. The attendants gave her some plantains and
Nagamma went to the kitchen and brought some iddlies,
also for her. Lakshmi was satisfied and went away to her usual
place.

After the attendants returned to the hall and sat down,
Bhagavan remarked, looking at the attendants, “Do not all
of you come to me to relate your troubles? She too has done the
same thing. Why then are you vexed with her for coming here
with mud on her? When we have troubles, do we consider
whether our clothes are all right or our hair is properly
brushed?” 15

After Lakshmi’s Samadhi, Bhagavan related to Suri
Nagamma how the goshala was built:

“After Mother came to stay with me, regular cooking
and meals started, and after Lakshmi came, cattle and
dairying became established. Subsequently for three or four
years Lakshmi was presenting us with a calf every year on
the Jayanti day. Afterwards, that practice stopped.
Altogether she had nine deliveries. After Lakshmi came
here to stay, cows from different places were brought by
devotees and left here. So the cattle shed grew in size. In
the beginning they were tied up here and there under a
thatched shed. When Salem Sundaram Chetty (Judge)
came here, he decided to construct a goshala (cowshed)
and fixed an auspicious time for the laying of the
foundation stone. Half an hour before appointed time,



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 43

when everything was being got ready, Lakshmi broke loose
from her tether and came to me running as though to tell
me that a house was being constructed for her and that I
should be there. When I got up, she led me on to the spot.
Somehow she used to understand everything. She was very
smart indeed!” 16

After the inauguration ceremony was over, and every
one had left, Bhagavan talked to Annamalai Swami who was
doing the Ashram construction work at that time and told
him that the original plan must be changed, since many more
cows would come to the Ashram in the future and a larger
building would have to be built. 17

Lakshmi’s goshala was a very spacious and sturdy
building. The walls were built with dressed granite stones.
The entrance to the goshala was through a veranda on either
side of which were store-rooms. The interior design was
unique. The veranda led to an open-air courtyard for sunlight
and aeration. The centre of the courtyard, which was open
to the sky, was closed with iron grills because there was a
thick jungle behind the Ashram in those days. Bhagavan
made sure to close the courtyard with iron grills so that
leopards would not jump into the courtyard and take away
the calves. Beams made of teak wood from Burma supported
the roof and elegantly contoured stone columns supported
the roof in the courtyard. Granite flooring was laid to make
the cleaning easier. The inner wall of the courtyard was lined
with troughs where fodder was put for cows to eat. There
were water tanks to store water. At intervals there were pegs
to tie the cows. The courtyard led to a closed veranda like



44 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

hall on the other side where the calves were tied. Both the
front and the back door of the building had strong thick
teak wood frames and doors.

Bhagavan felt that by serving Lakshmi, the Ashram
would grow and prosper. He said, “If one builds this cow-shed
for Lakshmi, one will get necessary Punya to build a book store,
a dining room and a shrine for Matrubhuteswara.” 18

On the day of ceremonial opening of the goshala it was
decorated with festoons, mango leaves, flowers and kolams
drawn in front of the goshala. It was decided by the Ashram
people, that Lakshmi, the queen of the cows, would be the
first one to enter the goshala, for which an auspicious time
was fixed. Lakshmi was given a bath, decorated with turmeric,
vermilion and flower garlands to enter her new abode. But
suddenly Lakshmi slipped away and went to Bhagavan, who
was reclining on his sofa in the hall. She sat in front of him
and would not budge until he went with her. Bhagavan got
up and started for the goshala, Lakshmi trailed behind him.
Bhagavan entered her palace first, and she stepped in behind
him. The other cows and then the devotees followed them.19

Every Friday, after the bath, Lakshmi would be decorated
with turmeric on her forehead and adorned with kumkum
(vermilion) and with fresh flower garlands around her neck
and horns. Then Lakshmi would then come to the hall to
show her master, Bhagavan, her decoration. As soon as
Bhagavan touched her, she would stand in a trance without
moving. Sometimes she would start licking the hands of
Bhagavan with her rough tongue. Bhagavan would not
withdraw his hand even when his hand would become red



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 45

and blistered. He would let Lakshmi lick to her heart’s content
and return to her abode after receiving her share of prasad,
the hill plantains.20

On the day of Maattu Pongal Lakshmi was decorated
with flower garlands, turmeric, vermillion and eatables that
were made into garlands. She looked like a queen,
Kamadhenu the celestial cow. Bhagavan came to goshala at
10.0’clock, to shower his blessings on his cattle devotees. He
sat in a chair by the side of Lakshmi, who was beautifully
decorated. The devotees did Aarathi with camphor, chanting
Vedic hymns such as ‘Na Karmana’. Some of the devotees
said that they wanted to take a photograph of Lakshmi thus
decorated with Bhagavan. She was then led to the middle of
the goshala and the devotees who had gathered there in a
big group were asked to step aside. Lakshmi stood there tossing
her head gracefully. Bhagavan got up and stood by the side
of Lakshmi patting her head and back with his left hand.
When he said, “Steady, be steady Lakshmi!” Lakshmi closed
her eyes and remained absolutely quiet as if she was in
Samadhi. Bhagavan placed his right hand on her back and
with his walking stick in his left hand, stood by her side. It
was a divine vision. The photographer took two or three
photographs. Another photograph was taken when
Bhagavan was feeding Lakshmi with fruits and sweets. There
is another photograph where one can see Bhagavan sitting
in the midst of a herd of cows. It was a great day. All these
photographs can be seen in the Ashram even today.21

Thus, Lakshmi continued through the years as one of
the favoured devotees of Sri Bhagavan. The constant



46 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

association of the early years gradually became unnecessary
and occasional visits sufficed to sustain the flow of his Grace
(same as in the case of some human devotees). Whenever she
visited him he would pay attention to her, pat and stroke her
and feed her with plantains, rice cakes and sweet rice. She
was particular about her food. She did not much like ordinary
plantains, so whenever she came, Bhagavan would show great
solicitude and say, “Go and see if there are some hill-plantains”
and the attendants would run about attending to the needs
of his devotee.

Lakshmi’s great devotion and the possessive way in which
she always approached Bhagavan, and the great kindness and
attention he showed to her, convinced many devotees that
there was some special bond between them and that although
Lakshmi was now in the form of a cow, she must have attained
his Grace by love and surrender in her previous birth itself.
It was hard to explain in any other way the great solicitude
and tenderness Bhagavan always showed to her. Although
Bhagavan was all love and kindness and had concern for all,
he was rarely demonstrative of it. In contrast, the open
expressions of Grace that Lakshmi received from him were
quite exceptional.

Indeed, many of those who had been for a long time in
close association with Bhagavan and with the life of the
Ashram, believed that Lakshmi was a reincarnation of
Keeraipatti, the ‘Old Lady of the Greens’, who had known
Bhagavan from his earliest days at Tiruvannamalai and had
shown very great devotion to him during the years at
Virupaksha Cave. She served him in all such ways as she could



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 47

probably afford and occasionally prepared food for him
almost up to her death in 1921. Bhagavan never stated in so
many words, that Lakshmi was this old lady. Nevertheless,
the belief was supported by various spontaneous or casual
remarks of his when the circumstances gave rise to them.
His constant insistence that the Self is neither born nor reborn
and his injunction to realise the Self behind the illusion of
birth, death, and rebirth explains why he would never say
openly that such and such a person was reborn. It is, therefore,
not surprising that no one can quote any statement by
Bhagavan about Lakshmi and Keeraipatti although many
who heard Bhagavan refer to the two on various occasions
felt almost certain that they were the same and that the great
devotion of the old lady had caused her to return in this
humble guise to work out her remaining karma at the feet of
Bhagavan. 22

On January 26th, 1946 Bhagavan was in a reminiscent
mood and gave the following account of Keeraipatti to his
devotees:

Keeraipatti was already living in the big
Arunachaleswara Temple in the town when I first came
to Tiruvannamalai. She was staying at the Subramanya
temple in the big temple’s premises and she was feeding
Sadhus. At that time Keeraipatti had matted locks.
Later, when I went to live in Virupaksha cave on the
slopes of Arunachala hill, Keeraipatti lived in Guha
Namasivayar temple and had shaved off her hair. She
lived in the Mantapam of that temple. After getting up
in the morning, she would go for a walk on the hill and



48 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

collect fuel and cow dung. She would carry the dried
sticks on her back, cow dung in her hand and collect all
kinds of green leaves for cooking. She had only one pot
and she would boil water in it to take bath. Then she
would cook some rice in it. Keeping it aside on a leaf,
cook sauce for the rice. Everything was cooked in the
same pot. She would perform puja for the deities which
were on the walls and pillars of the Mantapam and
also did Naiveidyam i.e. offering the food to those deities.
Then she would give me the offered food. She would only
eat her food after I ate. In the evening, she would go to
the town to beg.

She was very attached to me and loved me too.
Sometimes, I would go and gather leaves or wild vegetables
that grow in the forest. I would help her in cooking by
cleaning and cutting vegetables. She never failed to bring
food to me. She loved me and supplied me food every day.
She died before we came here, and that is before 1922.
She was buried just near here, under a tamarind tree,
opposite Dakshinamurthi temple near the Ashram.23

Another day Bhagavan mentioned that both cow
Lakshmi and Keeraipatti hailed from the same place
Gudiyatham. Many times, when Bhagavan fed Lakshmi her
favourite food of hill plantains with his own hands, with love
and solicitude, the devotees felt that he was repaying the food
given to him by Keeraipatti.

Lakshmi lived in her new home, until she passed away
in 1948. In later years she came less and less to the hall.
Bhagavan visited her every day in the goshala. Lakshmi



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 49

would have preferred to stay more with Bhagavan, but in
the 1940’s the Ashram was so crowded, it was not practical
for Lakshmi to charge into the hall. In the last few years of
her life, she had to be content with the regular visits that
Bhagavan made to her.24

Cow Lakshmi’s final moments are recorded in her
‘Letters’ as below by the devotee Suri Nagamma who was
present at the time of Lakshmi’s Nirvana:

“To this queen, as for his own mother, Bhagavan
on Friday the 18th instant gave Videha Mukti
(deliverance from the body). That morning when I
went to the Ashram, I was told that Lakshmi was
seriously ill and would not survive the day. So I went
straight to the cowshed, without seeing Bhagavan
even. The room built for the calves was vacated,
cleaned and Lakshmi was given a bed of straw to lie
down upon. As it was Friday, she was as usual
decorated with turmeric paste, vermilion mark on the
forehead and a garland of flowers round the neck and
horns. Venkatratnam was sitting by the side fanning
her. Lakshmi was lying down with her majestic look
spreading lustre all round. She reminded me of
Kamadhenu going to Kailas to do Abhishekam with
milk over the great Lord Siva.

“When I went to Bhagavan and prostrated before
him and got up, he looked at me with a divine look. Taking it
as an order, I said I would go and stay with Lakshmi. He
nodded his head in assent and I went immediately.
Venkatratnam gave me the fan and left. Sitting in that



50 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

place I began repeating Ramana Dwadasakshari (twelve
letters of Ramana Mantram), Ashtotharam (108 names of
Ramana) etc. and Lakshmi appeared to hear them
carefully.

“Bhagavan came to the cowshed at 9-45 am as usual
to see Lakshmi. Bhagavan sat on the hay by her side, lifted
her head with both his hands, and passing one hand lightly
over her face and throat, and then placing his left hand
on the head, and began pressing with the right hand
fingers her throat right down to the heart. After pressing
like that for about a quarter of an hour he said, addressing
Lakshmi, ‘What do you say, mother? Do you want me to stay
here alone? I could stay, but what to do? All people could be
round you as in the case of my mother. Even so, why? Shall I
go?’ Lakshmi remained calm, devoid of all the bonds of
this world and of the pains of her body as though she
were in Samadhi. Bhagavan sat there unwilling to move
and with a heart full of compassion. I was overwhelmed
at the sight and exclaimed involuntarily, ‘Oh! Mother
Alagammal had the greatest luck. So has Lakshmi now.’
Bhagavan looked at me with a smile. Subramaniam came
and said, ‘It seems the doctor will not be coming till 10-30
as there is no immediate danger to Lakshmi.’

“‘All right, so doctor will not be coming now. Have you
brought the medicine for injection?’ asked Bhagavan.
Turning towards Lakshmi and gently stroking her head
and neck, he said, ‘What do you say? May I go?’
Subramaniam said, ‘She will feel happy if Bhagavan is by
her side.’ ‘That is so, but what to do?’ So saying and looking



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 51

into the eyes of Lakshmi, Bhagavan said, ‘What? May I go?
Won’t you tell me?’ Lakshmi looked at him proudly. What
reply Bhagavan got, we do not know but he got up and
went away saying, ‘See that the flies do not get into the mouth.’
I assured him that we would take due care of Lakshmi
and Bhagavan left the place very reluctantly.

“With the divine touch of Bhagavan, the outer breath
of Lakshmi began subsiding and the movement of the body
began to decrease. The doctor came at 10-30 am and gave
an injection. Lakshmi remained unaffected as if the body
was not hers. There was no death agony. Her sight was
calm and clear. The doctor turned her over into the
posture of Nandi, put some medicine on the boils and
went away instructing us to keep some support for the
head. It was 11-30 am. By then, Venkataratnam came back
after having his meal. He asked me to hold up the head
saying he would bring some more hay. The tongue
touched me and it was icy cold; the life of Lakshmi had
reached the feet of Sri Ramana and was absorbed in him.

“Ten minutes later, Bhagavan came into the shed
saying, ‘Is it all over?’ and squatted by her side, took her
face in both his hands as though she were a little child,
and lifted it and said, ‘Oh Lakshmi, Lakshmi’ and then, to
us, controlling his tears, he said, ‘Because of her, our family
(i.e. the Ashram) has grown to this extent.’ When all were
praising Lakshmi, Bhagavan asked, ‘I suppose the doctor
has not troubled her much, did he? How did her life cease?’
We told him all that had happened. ‘That is all right. Did
you notice this? The right ear is uppermost now. Till yesterday



52 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

she was lying down on her other side. Because of the boil she
was turned over to this side. So this ear had to come up.
Look, in the case of people who die in Kasi people say Lord
Siva will whisper into the right ear. Lakshmi too has her
right ear up,’ said Bhagavan, and showed that ear to all
people there. By that time, crowds gathered. After a
quarter of an hour, Bhagavan got up and said,
‘Ramakrishna has been saying for the last ten days that a
good tomb (samadhi) must be built for Lakshmi.’ Bhagavan
then went away to the hall.”25

Suri Nagamma recounts the burial of cow Lakshmi:
“All was ready by 6:00 pm. People had come in large

crowds. The Ashram authorities had decided to build a
Samadhi for the cow Lakshmi near the back wall of the
Ashram along with Valli the deer, the blessed crow and
Jackie the dog. Sadashiva Iyer who knew how to construct
a Samadhi had come to the Ashram two days before. He
supervised the whole construction, giving all the directions.
Bhagavan said, ‘It is individual luck! For Lakshmi, the
Samadhi is going to be erected on a scale equal to a Mahatma.’

“The Sarvadhikari brought the body of Lakshmi in a
wooden cart. Bhagavan came and sat on a chair. Lakshmi’s
body was placed opposite to him. Devotees brought water
in pots and poured it over the body of Lakshmi. After
that, abhishekam was done with milk, curds, ghee, sugar
and rose water. Incense was burnt; the body was covered
with a silk cloth. The face was smeared with turmeric
powder and vermilion and flower garlands were laid over
her body. Sugar candy was offered and aarati (waving of



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 53

lights) was performed. Lakshmi’s face beamed with beauty
and charm when she was thus decorated.

“By about 7 pm. devotees lowered the body of
Lakshmi into the grave with cries of, ‘Hara Hara
Mahadev’. Bhagavan was visibly affected. After Bhagavan
had touched the holy leaves, the Sarvadhikari threw them
on to Lakshmi. After that, devotees sprinkled turmeric
and vermilion powder, camphor, holy ash, sandal paste,
flowers and salt, and finally, earth was thrown in to fill the
grave. After the burial was over, Bhagavan came back to
the hall. The prasad was then distributed. The whole thing
ended like a marriage festival. Lakshmi the cow is no longer
in the cowshed. She has been freed from the bonds of the
body and is now merged in the lustrous Atman of Sri
Bhagavan Ramana.”26

In the early morning hours of the night Bhagavan
wrote an epitaph in Tamil for Lakshmi. At our request he
wrote it in Telugu and Malayalam also. Here is the Telugu
verse:

Venba:
Sri Sarvadhari Samvatsara Jyeshtakhya
Masasita Dvadasim Bhargava Vasaramu
Taraka Visakha sahitamm avu Lakshmigati
Cheru Dinamani Cheppu.” 27

It translates into English thus:
On Friday, the fifth of Ani, in the year
Sarvadhari, the twelfth day of the waxing moon,
Under the asterism Vishakha,
The cow Lakshmi attained mukthi.28



54 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

G.V. Subbaramayya noted, “It is a unique honour for a cow”.
Bhagavan had not sung like this even at his mother’s demise.”29

The Sarvadhikari directed that the verse be engraved
on a black stone as an epitaph for Lakshmi’s Samadhi. A
Samadhi shrine is built above the grave and a statue of
Lakshmi installed there.

Devaraja Mudaliar during his visit to the Ashram read the
above epitaph on the Samadhi and asked Bhagavan whether his
use of the word Vimukti (liberation) was just conventional as
when we say that someone has attained ‘samadhi’ meaning that
he has died, or whether it really meant ‘Nirvana.’

Bhagavan replied that it meant Nirvana (liberation).30

Bhagavan often visited the goshala and would enquire
about the cows there. He knew which were Lakshmi’s calves
but none of them came running to Bhagavan as Lakshmi
used to.

Once when Suri Nagamma went to the goshala with
Bhagavan, she asked him, “Bhagavan, did Lakshmi’s ultimate
aim in her previous life to progress in the spiritual path of
liberation, her good deeds and intense sadhana bring her to
you, in her present life as cow Lakshmi?”

Bhagavan answered, “Yes that is the truth. Her good
karmas of her previous life, diligence in sadhana, craving for
liberation brought her to us in her present life as the cow Lakshmi.
It was because of her good deeds, which alone bring the Guru
and Shishya together, that she came here. It is not an accident
but divine will.” 31

Every Friday morning puja is done at Lakshmi’s samadhi.
A special puja is offered on the day of her samadhi and on
Maattu Pongal day.



IN MEMORIAM
The Holy Cow Lakshmi Passed into the Light on

18 June, 1948 at 11.30 am.
Harindranath Chattopadhyaya

Before the Golden Sage who reigneth still
At the wide foot of the celestial Hill
Arunachala-lord of the Beacon Light,
I bow in reverence, and then rise to write
This song about the Cow who, ere she died,
Was by His touch of mercy deified
And set among the Immortals who continue
To breathe as Light within me and within you.

Lord of Grace! thy holy Name resounds
From end to end; thy Mercy knows no bounds,
Thy Power no limitation! Through thy Peace
The struggles of thy seekers slowly cease
Leaving a large contentment in the heart:
Before thy luminous Presence glooms depart,
Clouds vanish ... In the stillness of thine eye
The all-unseeing fool grows sudden wise,
The ignorant grow learned. With a smile
Thou canst redeem us in a fleeting while,
Rendering our lives significant. O thou
Who wearest realisation on thy
Even as a jewel! With what master-ease
Thou dost immortalise thy devotees!

And thou art worshipped everywhere by all
Who, touched by thy deep Grace, have heard thee

call
And gathered at thy Feet: numerous shapes
Of peacocks, squirrels, deer, and dogs and apes,



56 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Of cows and men. And from them thou dost draw
Thy chosen few according to a Law
Known but to thee, whom, drenching in thy Love
Thou dost, to each, allot a height above
Earth’s little level, that they may arise
From hells of flesh to the soul’s paradise.

It is for her I sing who is no more
Lakshmi, the Master’s sacred Cow, who bore
The beauty of a goddess - she who was
Experience carven out of luminous pause
And moulded into creature line and curve;
Lakshmi, the Mother Cow, was born to serve

Sage Ramana

Yet, it was nothing strange,
Some say her creature form was but a change
From human, since - such is the story told -
She was a woman once, wizened and old,
Her wrinkled body all in tatters clad,
But held behind that ugliness she had
A lamplike soul that bade her self engage
In long and selfless service of the sage.

She came to Him through many noons and eves
Bringing Him simple fare of herbs and leaves
Plucked with devotion, cooked with love and care,
And, it is said, He ate the humble fare
As though it were a banquet!

Serving thus
She died and passed into the luminous
Lakshmi that she might serve Him once again
And so, through service, finally attain
Self-knowledge and release….



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 57

Great Master! thou
Art all-compassionate. Upon the brow
Of self-surrender thou dost seal thy Grace
And dost, in a miraculous while, efface
Bondage and grief. In thy compassion’s ken
Dumb creatures share an equal place with men.

Thy law is universal, working out
Even through layers and layers of lampless doubt
Ultimate faith which sees thee as thou art:
Master Illumination of the heart!

Even so did Lakshmi know thee, Master! When
She breathed and moved amidst a world of men
Who scarcely know thee. Even as a cloud
Moves in the wide horizon, glow-endowed
And solitarily she moved with Grace
Within thy Love’s horizonal embrace;

Within thy Mercy’s garden, hour by hour,
She grew from bud to flower, and then from flower
Into the ripened fruit of wisdom hued
With subtle hues of inward solitude.
And while she was a bud she lit the air
With delicate sweetness making us aware
Of some high mission to be done through her;
Then, in the flowering state, she seemed to stir
The hermitage with more-than-human power,
And everywhere she went she was a flower
Scattering fragrance drawn from inwardness;
The Master met her soul, beyond our guess,
In high communion and absolute
Love that transformed the flower into the fruit
Of ultimate ripe attainment.



58 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

She has passed
Into His shining vast
Of Essence, beyond form and name;
She has become a Flame
Upon His quiet altar which shall burn
Forevermore, lending at every turn
Light to our hearts and splendour to our minds; Dark
Death, the wind of winds,
Cannot disturb it on that altar burning:
She will no more in any form occur,
The law of evolution no more binds
her great untrammelled spirit. Lo, for her
There is no more returning!

Yet, let us see what was the heritage
Which brought Her to the all-compassionate Sage?
What strange unearthly scope
Embodied in her horoscope
And in herself? this creature made of earth,
What gave the high significance to her birth?
What penance and what prayer
In other births than this, her last,
Did she perform, scaling stair upon stair
Of pure illumination in the past
Closing in ultimate ripeness?

Let us unveil
before the world her fascinating tale:
In nineteen twenty six, four mortal years
After the passing of the Sage’s Mother
Into the State of Light and Liberation,
Granted to Her by the great Seer of Seers,
A bhakta came to offer salutation
To Him, and brought with him a gift along,



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 59

A cow and a she-calf, lovely and strong
The rose-red season-ache in blood and limb, With
tenderness the creatures gazed on Him
And knew He was their Lord...

He only smiled
And gently said: “The Mother and her child Need
tending with devotion, love and care.
So, take them back, and know that I am there
Wherever they may be ...”

*     *     *

Arunachala Pillai
“Lord, they are thine,

Have mercy and accept them. Every line
Contour and curve of them are thine alone.
It is a gift I make thee, humbly bowed
In reverence. I pray, do not disown
The humble gift....”

Out of the crowd
Emerged a little man, who hardly spoke
At other times, a puny fragile man
Whose words collected to a master-stroke
And seemed thrice pregnant with some future plan
Of which he was unconscious.

*     *     *

Ramanatha Dikshitar
It is my prayer

The offering be accepted in this place
They shall remain receiving love and care
Fed not on fodder merely, but Thy Grace!



60 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Thenceforth, the mother-cow remain beside
Her lovely calf which like a crescent waxed
In beauty day by day, and seemed untied
Knot after knot of creature hood; relaxed
Slowly but surely from her animal state
The calf appeared to sense the Lord and wait
Tip-toe on some great happening, all sublime:
Then, they were led after a little time,
To dwell within a dairy in the town
Among a hundred others of their ilk,
Conducted by a person of renown
Who earned his daily bread by selling milk.
His name was Pasupati Iyer ... he
Was Ramana’s thrice humble devotee
Who visited the Sage from day to day
And with him brought these twain to bend and Pay
Homage along with him.

The calf began
Within herself to calculate and plan
Her future carefully,-yet, no one guessed
The gathering inspirations in her breast!
Without show or fuss, she had decided
To learn by note the roadways that divided
The dairy from the hermitage. And soon,
Growing from the young crescent to the moon,
She grew in wisdom and self-confidence
And with her growth, within her grew the sense
Of adoration for the Lord who was
Already bending her to other laws
Than those of earth and time. Each nerve in her
Began to wake and ache and thrill and stir
With sweetness, until every nerve became



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 61

A roadway kindled with unearthly flame
Leading her footsteps to the hermitage
Where on her own she came at last to dwell!
One of the few, the chosen of the Sage
Held evermore under His magic spell.

Lakshmi they called her; and, indeed, she proved
Goddess of Wealth. The way she breathed and moved,
The manner of her gait, the light that beamed
In her large jewel-glorious eyeballs seemed
To bear an air of boons.

After her coming
The little hermitage began a-humming
With affluence and progress. Thus, at length,
It grew from joy to joy, from strength to strength,
Lands multiplied, grew plenty, laughed with grain
And structures loomed like links within the chain Of
Ramana’s growing Kingdom.
Her tangled mesh Of mortal life is over.
She is not Among us now, a form of blood and flesh,
But lives as lyric light that never dies; The memory
of her movements brimmed with Grace Haunts us
forever, and her jewel-eye
Glitter in every corner of this place
Shedding strange glory. Everywhere we turn We feel
her presence like to moonrise burn Cleaving our

darkness.

While they build and raise
A monument to her in humble praise
Of her existence and her saintliness,
She stands amidst the toiling men to bless Their
labour. Her divine arithmetic
Sums up the meaning of each stone and brick Which



62 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

go to build her pure mausoleum
Her Presence is not heralded by drum
Or cymbal nor announced by sounding pipe, But by
Itself which sheds around a ripe
Self-blaze of realisation. She remains
As one ecstatic tingle in our veins
Linking us with the Master, golden-hearted,
Who but a little hour ere she departed,
Touched her into release, - calm stroke by stroke
Thrice tenderly, compassionately, awoke
In her a wondrous seerhood!

She has gone
Beyond our common ken, beyond the dawn And noon
and evenfall-nay, she has earned Creation’s rich totality

and turned
Part of creation’s sweetness gripped above Desire and
hunger, thirst and ache and love which hold the world

in bondage.

Seer of Seers!
Perfect us difficultly through the years
Into the state to which she has attained;
Make us, like her, surrender, every inch
Until we, too, have reached thy Feet and gained
Self-cancellation. May we never flinch
Even by a hair’s-breadth from the Truth thou art:
Teach us, as thou didst her, in limb and heart
The full surrender until naught remains
Of blindness and corruption, rusted chains
Of thee-betrayal.

May we evermore
 Learn from sweet Lakshmi to salute thy Light
 To bend in self-surrender and adore



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 63

 Thy Presence reigning on the starry height
 Piercing the darks of sightless human sight.
 Give us the Grace, like her, to see
 Beyond thy human form, thy Majesty
 Enveloping the universe. Like her
 Make each of us a true interpreter
 Of spirit-radiance until we, who seek,
 Grow one and indivisible with thy Peak.

 (Archives of Sri Ramanasramam)

 2. THE BLACK COW
This is an account by Suri Nagamma in one of her letters,

of a cow, which waited for the holy and benevolent look of
Bhagavan, to take her last breath peacefully:

“For the last three days the black cow in the goshala was
suffering from some ailment. She was tied to a tree near the
shed built for the calves. Though she was suffering for three
days, Bhagavan did not chance to pass that way. Yesterday
she was in her last throes of death. Though she was suffering
since morning, she did not breathe her last till 5 pm. Bhagavan
got up from his couch in the hall at 4.45 pm. as usual to go
behind the goshala. While returning, he turned towards the
place where the cow was, stopped at the shed constructed
for the calves and watched the black cow’s agony for a while.
As Bhagavan was the embodiment of kindness, it was natural
that his heart melted with pity. He favoured the cow with a
look of deliverance from bondage, and then he came back
and sat as usual on his couch.

“After Bhagavan’s benign look fell on her, the Jiva
remained in the body for only five minutes. The black cow



64 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

had waited for Bhagavan’s benevolent look and as soon as
that was obtained, her Jiva left the body.

“It is said that if a person can think of God at the time of
death, that person gets freed from the bondage of Samsara.
How fortunate should that cow be that she should get freed
from the bondage of samsara at the time of her death by
Bhagavan’s holy and benevolent look!

“Bhagavan had told his devotees several times, that
though several animals suffered for days together, it never
occurred to him to look at them. But in some cases it suddenly
occurred to him to look at those suffering animals in their
agonies of death. He added in such cases, the animals passed
away peacefully and immediately afterwards. Devotees saw
an instance of this in the case of the Black Cow.

“There may be many more instances of this kind where
animals got liberation from Bhagavan. But we do not know
this, as they are not recorded unlike in the cases of Lakshmi
the cow, Valli the deer, Jackie the dog or the blessed crow.1

3. CALF - THE DEVOTEE
After Bhagavan’s Nirvana a devotee mentioned about

the incident of the devotion of a calf to Bhagavan:
“In the Ashram, there was a calf that used to visit the

houses of devotees of Bhagavan in Ramana Nagar. The
devotees welcomed her lovingly and gave her sweets and
fruits to eat.

“One day I was in Ramanasramam. My friend came
and told me that the calf was standing in my compound.
When I heard this, I was very happy. I rushed to my house
to feed the calf with fruits and sweets. As I did not see the



Bhagavan’s Devotees – The Cows 65

calf in the compound of my house, thinking that she may
be inside the house, I went inside the house and looked
for her, but she was not to be seen. When I entered my
bedroom, I saw the calf standing in front of the photograph
of Bhagavan fully immersed in it. Her eyes were fixed on
Bhagavan with love and devotion. She was bowing to
Bhagavan with her forehead touching the photograph of
Bhagavan. She had merged and become one with
Bhagavan at that moment.

“She did not even notice me entering the room. She
was oblivious of her surroundings. Seeing the love and
the devotion of the calf, I was enthralled but at the same
time, I felt ashamed of myself for the lack of the same
intensity of devotion that the calf had for Bhagavan. In
spite of me being a human being and in constant touch
with Bhagavan, not even once had I surrendered so
completely to Bhagavan!

“I waited in the hall for the calf to come out of the
bed room. When she came out, I fed her with sweets and
fruits. Who knows which great devotee of Bhagavan had
tenanted the body of the calf?”1



66 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation
