song 29).



CHAPTER – TWENTY ONE

BHAGAVAN - WHOM ALL BEINGS LOVED
Who are with equal eye, as lord of all
A hero perish or a sparrow fall
To him no high, no low, no great, no small

– Alexander Pope
(From Self Realization)

He prayeth best who loveth best
All things both great and small

– Samuel Taylor Coleridge
(From Self Realization)

One characteristic of Bhagavan that struck everybody,
including new visitors to the Ashram, was the way in which he
dealt with animals, especially the ones that lived in the Ashram.

After going through so many incidents about Bhagavan
and his animal and bird companions, one is awe struck at
the interaction and rapport he had with them and the way
the animals and birds responded to his love, care, compassion
and solicitude. Where did Bhagavan learn the languages of
birds and beasts? It was natural to him to know. He was a
Sarvajna, the all knower. For him, it may be a squirrel or a
cheetah, a big banyan tree or a small seed they were all the
same consciousness but with different garbs and it did not
matter what garb the jiva wore. It was the soul (Jiva) within
the garb which had come to his proximity to reap the fruits



Bhagavan – Whom All Beings Loved 265

of their past deeds including those of their previous births.
For him nature, the animals and birds were not just friends,
but also teachers through whose example he taught some of
the vendantic concepts and ways of life. Taking some examples
of their way of life, he gave guidance for mankind to follow.
To our Bhagavan there was no difference between man, beast
or bird. He treated them all with equal vision.

Bhagavan’s outlook on life was never worldly; his was
the loftiest peak of spiritual consciousness. From that mighty
height, everything, man, animal and plant (all animate and
inanimate things of the world) ware seen as the One, without
difference, as Ishwara the Lord.

As Pope says:
All are but parts of one stupendous whole
Whose body nature is, but God the soul;
That changed through all, and yet in all the same;
Great in the earth, as in the ethereal frame;
Warms in the sun, refreshes in the breeze.
Glows in the stars and blossoms in the tree,
Lives through all life, extends through all extent,
Spreads undivided, operates unspent;
Breathes in our soul, informs our mortal part,
As full as perfect in a hair, as heart:
As full as perfect in vile man that mourns,
As the rapt seraph that adores and burns;
To him, no high, no low, no great, no small
He fills; he bounds, connects and equals all.
Nothing is foreign, parts relate to whole,
One all extending, all-preserving soul
Connects each being, greatest with the least;
Made beast in aid of man, and man of beast:



266 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

All served, all serving; nothing stands alone;
The chain holds on; - and where it ends, unknown.

(From Self Realization)

The ideal set out by Lord Krishna for a wise man (sloka
18 of Chapter 5 of Bhagavad Gita) is:

“The wise (the knowers of Self ) look with equal eye
on a learned but humble brahmin, a cow, an elephant, a
dog and a chandala living on dog’s flesh.”

That is our Bhagavan. In fact eminent Pandits and the
Ashram dogs sat alike on the same floor before Bhagavan
and were equally attended to in Bhagavan’s presence. Neither
was preferred to the other by him in the matter of provision
of food, accommodation or attention and treatment during
distress or illness.

Bhagavan’s life in many ways was unique and all his own.
Bhagavan would see in everybody and in everything Sat-Chit-
Ananda. For him, from an atom to this Brahmanda, the
universe that is creation was Sarvam Brahma Mayam, the all
pervading Brahman. Knowing the self (Who am I?), the
Atman is Brahma Jnanam. When this knowledge is realized,
there is no superior or inferior Jeevas in creation. This
knowledge gives equanimity – of mind and vision. Our Guru
Bhagavan took the human form to guide us in the path of
Self realization.

We are blessed to have him as our inner Guru.
As the devotee Desikananda writes:
“How gracious art thou Lord Dakshinamurthi,
To have blessed mankind by thy ministration in

human form,



Bhagavan – Whom All Beings Loved 267

To Bhagavan who is but the form of Satyam, Sivam
and Sundaram,

Do I offer my salutations again and again”.2

The devotee G.V. Subbaramayya has composed the
following couple of verses seeking Bhagavan’s Grace:

“Seeing you caress peacocks, squirrels, cows,
dogs, monkeys and children with such tender
Grace, any one must melt to his bones, O Ramana!

“So many birds and animals coming to you have
attained deliverance.

Likewise do bless with Grace this human animal
That has sought refuge at your feet, O Ramana 3

This prayer is for all the devotees of Bhagavan who come
to him.

SOURCE
Content Reference

1. Bhagavan Sri Ramana 1 Self Realization by B.V.Narasimha
Maharshi - All Beings Loved Swamy.

2 Face to face with Bhagavan, Page 212,
Published by Ramana Kendra,
Hyderabad.

3 Sri Ramana Reminiscences by G.V.
Subbaramayya, Page 136.



ANNEXURE

Bhagavan was Sarvajna. He shone as the Lord
Dakshinamurthi, through silence proclaiming the reality of
the Truth. But still sometimes he had to explain to the seeker
by using the examples of plant, beast, or birds in nature to
teach the reality of the Truth.

Here are some of the incidents in which he taught
devotees and seekers with nature as example.

1. WALK WITH BHAGAVAN TO THE LAKE
T.K.Sundaresa Iyer (T.K.S) one of Bhagavan’s staunch

devotees came to Bhagavan as a boy of twelve. Later he became
a school teacher and stayed near Bhagavan. the following
incidentis takenfrom his book At the feet of Bhagavan about :

Once long ago the Samudram Lake at the foot of the
Arunachala hill which is near Ramanasramam overflowed. The
sight of it was very grand and the overflow was as wide as a
river. The tank really seemed like the sea of its name
(Samudram). Bhagavan told that it got this name Samudram
because certain local ruler had this tank constructed as
Miniature Sea to give his queen an idea of what a sea would
look like; for she had never seen the sea and wished to do so.

One morning the devotees in the hall expressed to
Bhagavan a desire to visit the lake, and he was kind enough
to accept the request. Bhagavan with his devotees went to
see the overflowing lake which is about a mile (1.6 km) from
the Ashram. The presence of Bhagavan and his words were



Annexure 269

more interesting to devotees than the brimming tank and the
grand view of the wide water at the foot of the holy Arunachala.

At one place he pointed out a Palmyra tree which had
decayed in the embrace of a parasitic banyan tree. Some bird
had dropped a banyan seed into the Palmyra tree and as it
began to grow, the Palmyra tree became cloven and stunted
in its own growth. Drawing our attention to this phenomenon,
Bhagavan remarked that this is just what the look of Grace
from a Jnani does. One look into the soul, and the whole
tree of past tendencies and prejudices (vasana) gathered up
through long cycles of past births would burn up and decay.
Then the reality of the Self would be experienced. Thus
Bhagavan explained to us the effect of contact with the great
and said that the supreme Jnana obtained with the touch of
the saint can never be obtained through the study of any
number of scriptures, or by any store of good deeds, or by
any other spiritual practices and efforts. Later, on return to
the Ashram, T.K.S put this in verse form as below:

 A bird drops seed upon a tree and causes its decay.
 So Guru’s Grace rays knowledge into the seeking

mind.

 Replacing ego – shadows with resplendent Jnana’s
light.

The essence of this verse, which is in Tamil, is that made
by Bhagavan himself. The seed of the huge Banyan tree,
which grows to shelter hundreds of beings, is one of the tiniest
and represents unselfish benevolence. The seed of the Palmyra
which is so large grows into a tree which can hardly shelter a



270 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

single being from the sun, and so well represents the selfish ego.
Yet this tiny seed can be dropped by a bird in its droppings, and
while it grows it can demolish the Palmyra tree itself. So the tiny
seed of Grace of the Guru can destroy the great tree of egoism.

After marvelling at the width of the overflowing water
at the outlet at the end of the lake, Bhagavan and the
devotees walked back on the bund of the lake. They
happened to pass the sluice at the centre of the bund.
Pointing to this Bhagavan remarked:

“‘Look at this small outlet, as compared with the
big one at the end! But for this small hole, through
which the stream of water trickles, the vast contents of
the lake would not be helpful to vegetation. If the bund
breaks it will be regular deluge, and the entire crop
will be destroyed. Only by the water served under proper
regulation through this sluice, the plants can grow. So
is with divine consciousness too. Unless the bliss of this
consciousness is gifted through the Grace of the Guru
in controlled outlets, the soul cannot be helped to the
destruction of its tendencies of the past.’”

SOURCE
Content Reference

1. Walk with Bhagavan to the Lake “At the Feet of Bhagavan”, T K
Sundaresa Iyer, Page 38.



Annexure 271

 The following are excerpts’ from the book Talks with
Ramana Maharshi by Munagala Venkataramaiah

1. HEN AND CHICKS (TALK – 286)
A devotee asked Bhagavan:
“How do we go to sleep and how do we wake up?”
Bhagavan replied:

“Just at nightfall the hen clucks and the chicks go and
hide themselves under her wings. Then the hen goes to roost
in the nest with the chicks in her protection. At dawn the
chicks come out, so does the hen. The mother-hen stands for
the ego which collects all the thoughts (the chicks) and goes
to sleep. At sunrise the rays emerge forth and are collected
again at sunset. Similarly when ego displays itself, it does so
with all its paraphernalia, that is waking. When it sinks,
everything disappears with it. That is sleep. – Sushupti.”

2. CATERPILLAR (TALK 286)
How to experience ego in its purity?
Bhagavan explained:

“The ego in its purity is experienced in intervals between
two states or two thoughts. Ego is like that caterpillar which
leaves its hold only after catching another. The ego’s true nature
can be found when it is out of contact with the object or thoughts.
Realize this interval with the conviction gained by the study of
avasthatraya (The three states of consciousness – Waking,
Dreaming, and Sleeping)”

3. MOTH-LIKE INSECT (TALK 616)
“The world is within and not without. This meaning is

contained in verses 11 & 12, Chapter V of Ramana Gita .



272 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

“The entire Universe is condensed in the body and the
entire body in the Heart. Thus the Heart is the nucleus of the
whole universe.” Therefore Samyamana relates to
concentration on different parts of the body for different
siddhis. Also the Visva or the Virat is said to contain the
cosmos within the limits of the body. Again The world is not
other than the mind, the mind is not other than the Heart.
That is the whole truth.” So Heart comprises all. This is what
is taught to Swetaketu by the illustration of the seed of a fig
tree. The source is a point without any dimensions. It expands
as the cosmos on one hand and as infinite bliss on the other.
That point is the pivot. From it a single vasana starts,
multiplies as the experiencer ‘I’, the experience, and the world.
The experience and the source are referred to in the mantra.
Two birds, exactly alike, arise simultaneously.”

For this Bhagavan gave an example:
“When I was staying in Skandasramam, I sometimes

used to go out and sit on a rock. On one such occasion
there were two or three others with me including
Rangaswamy Iyengar. Suddenly we noticed some small
moth- like insect shooting up like a rocket into the air
from the crevice in the rock. Within the twinkling of an
eye it had multiplied itself into millions of moths which
formed a cloud and hid the sky from view. We wondered
at it and examined the place from which it shot up. We
found that it was only a pin hole and knew that so many
insects could not have issued from it in such a short time.

“That is how Ahankara (ego) shoots up like a rocket
and instantaneously spreads out as the universe.”



Annexure 273

“The Heart is therefore the centre. A person can never
be away from it. If he is he is already dead. Although the
Upanishads say that the jiva functions through other centres
on different occasions, yet he does not relinquish the Heart.
The centres are simply places of business (vide Vedanta
Chudamani). The Self is bound to the Heart, like a cow
tethered to a peg. The movements are controlled by the length
of the rope. All its wanderings centre around the peg.

“A caterpillar crawls on a blade of grass and when it
has come to the end, it seeks another support. While doing
so it holds on with its hind-legs to the blade of grass, lifts
the body and sways to and fro before it can hold another.
Similarly it is with the Self. It stays in the Heart and
holds other centres also according to circumstances. But
its activities always centre round the Heart.”

4. THE STRAY BULL (TALK - 563)
A group of people came on a visit to Bhagavan. One of

them asked, “How can I keep my mind aright?”
Bhagavan replied: “A refractory bull is lured to the

stall by means of grass. Similarly the mind must be lured
by good thoughts.”

Visitor: “But it does not remain steady”.
Bhagavan: “The bull accustomed to straying takes

delight in going astray. However, he must be lured with
luscious grass to the stall. Even so he will continue to trespass
into the neighbours’ fields. He must gradually be made to
realize that the same kind of good grass can be had in his
own place. After a time he will remain in the stall without
straying. Later a time will come when, even if driven out



274 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

of the stall, he will return to the stall without going to the
neighbouring fields. So also the mind must be trained to
take the right ways. It will gradually grow accustomed to
good ways and will not return to wrong ways”.

Devotee: “What are the good ways to be shown to the
mind?”

Bhagavan: “Thought of God.”

5. ELEPHANT AND THE CHAIN (TALK – 326)
When a devotee complained about his restless mind,

Bhagavan said:
“Everybody complains of the restlessness of the mind.

Let the mind be found and then they will know. True,
when a man sits down to meditate thoughts rush up by
dozens. The mind is only a bundle of thoughts. The attempt
to push through the barrage of thoughts is unsuccessful. If
one can by any means abide in the Self it is good. For
those who are unable to do so, chanting or meditation
(Japa or Dhyana) is prescribed.

“It is like giving a piece of chain to an elephant to
hold in its trunk. The trunk of an elephant is usually
restless. It puts it out in all directions when taken out in
the streets of the town. If given a chain to carry the
restlessness is checked. Similarly it is with the restless mind.
If it is made to engage in Japa or Dhyana, other thoughts
are warded off. The mind concentrates on a single thought.
It thus becomes peaceful. It does not mean that peace is
gained without a prolonged struggle. The other thoughts
must be fought out.”



Annexure 275

DROPS OF NECTAR FROM BHAGAVAN
When the seeker had genuine doubts in his spiritual

sadhana, sometimes Bhagavan would clear those doubts by
using examples from thhe animal and plant kingdoms to make
the point clearer to the seeker.

Here are some of them taken from Talks with Sri Ramana
Maharshi written by Munagala Venkataramaiah who kept a
diary of daily events that took place in Bhagavan’s hall.

1. Talk 398 (Page 445): Bhagavan:
The ego is a powerful elephant and cannot be

brought under control by anyone less than a lion, who is
no other than the Guru in this instance, whose look makes
the elephant (ego) tremble and die. We will know in due
course that our glory lies where we cease to exist.’”

2. Talk 617 (Page 704): Bhagavan while describing
Avastatraya, the three states of consciousness, said:

“The vegetable kingdom is always in sushupti; the
animals have both swapna and sushupti; the Gods (the
celestials) are always in jagrat; man has all the three states;
but the clear sighted Yogi abides only in turiya, and the
highest Yogi remains in turiyatita alone.”

3. Talk 25 (Page 28): Bhagavan: “The world is
‘sensed’ in the waking and the dream states or is the object
of perception and thought, both being mental activities.
If there were no such activities as waking and dreaming
thought, there would be no ‘perception’ or inference of a
‘world’. In sleep there is no such activity and ‘objects and
world’ do not exist for us in the sleep. Hence reality of the
world may be created by the ego by the act of emergence



276 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

from sleep; and that reality may be swallowed up or
disappear by the soul resuming its nature in sleep. The
emergence and disappearance of the world are like
the spider producing a gossamer web and then
withdrawing it. The spider here underlines all three
states – waking, dreaming and sleep; such a spider in
the person is called Atman (Self ), whereas the same
with reference to the world (which is considered to issue
from the sun) is called Brahman (Supreme spirit). He
that is in man is the same as He that is in the sun.”

4. Talk 520 (Page 608): Bhagavan: “Any unreal thing
cannot produce effects. It is like saying that you killed some
animal with the horn of a hare. A hare does not grow horns.”

5. Talk 601 (Page 673): Devotee: “When we attempt to
cease from activity, the very attempt is action. So activity seems
to be inevitable.”

Bhagavan:“True, Thayumanavar has also alluded to it.
A doctor advises a patient to take the prescribed medicine
with only one condition. That condition is not to think of a
monkey when he takes the medicine. Can the patient ever
take the medicine? Will he not think of the monkey whenever
he tries not to do so?

So also, when people try to give up thoughts their
object is frustrated by their very attempt.”

6. Talk 17 (Page 16):Bhagavan:“Just as a plantain tree
produces shoots at its roots before yielding fruits and
perishing, and these shoots, being transplanted, do the same
again. So also the original primeval master of antiquity, Lord
Dakshinamurthi, who cleared the doubts of his Rishi disciples



Annexure 277

in silence, has left shoots which are ever multiplying. The
Guru is the shoot of that Dakshinamurthi.”

7. Talk 616 (Page 699): Bhagavan: “The relation
between the Self and the body or the mind may be compared
to that of a clear crystal and its background. If a crystal is
placed against a red flower, it shines red; if placed against a
green leaf, it shines green and so on. The individual confines
himself to the limits of the changeful body or mind which
derives existence from the unchanging self. All that is
necessary is to give up the mistaken identity, and that done,
the ever shining Self will be seen to be the single non-dual
Reality.”

8. Talk 108 (Page 123): Devotee asked: “How does the
wide universe sprout forth from such subtle samskaras
remaining sunk in the heart?’

 Bhagavan : “Just as a big banyan tree sprouts
from a tiny seed, so the wide universe with names and
forms sprouts forth from the heart.”

9. Talk 396 (Page 440): Bhagavan said: “Gold is
not an ornament, but the ornament is nothing but gold.
Whatever shapes the ornament may assume and however
different the ornaments are, there is only one reality, namely
gold. So also with the bodies and the Self. The single reality is
the Self. To identify oneself with the body and yet to seek
happiness is like attempting to cross a river on the back of an
alligator. The body identity is due to extroversion and the
wandering of the mind. To continue in that state will only
keep one in an endless tangle and there will be no peace.
Seek your source, merge in the Self and remain all alone.’”



278 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

10. Talk 538 (Page 622): Devotee: “What is the
exact difference between worldly activity and
Dhyana?’”

Bhagavan:“There is no difference. It is like naming
one and the same thing by two different words in two
different languages. The crow has two eyes but only one
iris which is rolled into either eye as it pleases. The elephant
has a trunk used for breathing and also drinking water.
The snake sees and hears with the same organ.”

11. Talk 399 (Page 449): Bhagavan:“They say that
the world is unreal. Of what degree of unreality is it? Is it
like that of a son of a barren mother or a flower in the
sky, mere words without any reference to facts? Whereas
the world is a fact and not a mere word. The answer is
that it is a superimposition on the one Reality, like the
appearance of a snake on a coiled rope seen in dim light.”

Om Sri Ramanarpanamastu



GLOSSARY

A
Aarathi :  The waving of lights before a sacred image
Abhayam: State of fearlessness.
Abhayam sarva bhuthebhyaha: security and compassion
    for all beings.
Abhishekam: The sacred bath of water, milk, curd, etc.,
    given to a deity
Agamya karma: The new karmas accumulated according to
    the good or bad deeds done by the human being, to be
    enjoyed later.
Ahimsa: Non violence.
Aksharamanamalai: Marital Garland of 108 verses composed on
    Lord Arunachala  by Bhagavan Sri Ramana.
Amma: Mother, an elderly woman.
Anna: Elder brother or an older person.
Aradhana day: Mahasamadhi day of a holy man. Nirvana day.
Arunachala: The Holy Hill on which Bhagavan Sri Ramana spent
    24 years from 1900 to 1922.
Arunachala Puranam: Ancient poems containing mythological
    stories, legends of  Lord  Arunachaleswara.
Ashram: The dwelling place of sages and ascetics.
Ashramites: The inmates who live in the Ashram.
Ashtakam: a hymn of praise in eight stanzas.
Ashtottaram : 108 names of Deity chanted during worship.
Atman: Soul, the Self of all beings.
Avatar: A Devine manifestation; incarnation of the God.

B
Bhagavan: The Lord; any great person with divine qualities;
    a blessed one.



280 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Bhakta: Devotee.
Bhakti: Devotion to God.
Bhakti marga: The path to God through love and devotion.
Bharatavarsha: India, the land once ruled by king Bharatha.
Bhikshu: Supplicant, Mendicant.
Bilwa tree: The sacred tree whose leaves are offered in the
    worship of Siva.
Brahma Jnanam: Realization of Brahman.
Brahman: The one formless, non dual absolute substratum
    of all that exists.
Brahmin: Member of the priestly caste among the Hindus.

C
Chidambaram: The town in Tamil Nadu famous for its
    Natraja Temple; literally, consciousness
    (chit) - space (ambaram).
Chit: Consciousness.

D
Darshan: Seeing a holy person or a deity.
Dharma: Code of social or personal morality.
Dhyana: Meditation or Contemplation.
Dikshitar: Brahmin who is an exponent in Shastras.
Dwadasakshari: Twelve syllable Mantra;
    e.g., OM NAMO BHAGAVATHE SRI RAMANAYA.

G
Giripradakshina: Going round the hill keeping it to one’s right.
Gokulashtami: The eighth lunar day of lunar month of
    Shravana on which Lord Krishna was born. It usually
    falls in the month of August.
Gomatha: the mother cow.
Goshala: Cowshed.
Guha: Cave.



Glossary 281

Guhai Namasivayar: the great devotee of Siva who lived in
    a cave on Arunachala hill later named after him.
Gunas: The three fundamental qualities, tendencies or
    stresses which underlie all manifestations: sattva,
    rajas and tamas.
Guru : Preceptor; a spiritual master.

H
Hara: Lord Siva.
Hari: Lord Vishnu.

I
Iddlies: Steamed cake made of lentils and rice.
Ishwara: God; the Supreme Being in his aspect of the
    Lord of the world.

J
Jayanti: celebration of the birthday of a great man,
Jiva: The individual.
Jivanmukti: Liberation while one is alive.
Jnana: Spiritual knowledge, wisdom.
Jnani: Sage; one who has realized the Self.
Jubilee Hall: A thatched pandal abutting the old hall,
    facing the hill which was erectedduring the Golden
    Jubilee Celebration of Bhagavan’s advent to Arunachala
    in the Ashram.
Jyoti: Effulgence.

K
Kailasa: The abode of Lord Siva in mountain Himalayas.
Kali yuga: The last of the four yugas (aeons).
Kalpataru: Kalpavruksha; The Wish fulfilling celestial tree.
Kamadhenu: The Wish fulfilling celestial cow.
Kamandalu: A receptacle used by ascetics for holding water
    and generally made out of  the shell of a large coconut.



282 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Kannadiga: The one who hails from Karnataka, a south Indian
    State, speaking Kannada.
Karma: destiny based on cause and effect.
Karpura arati: the waving of lighted camphor during puja.
Karuna: Compassion.
Karunapoorna Sudhabdhi: Ocean full of compassion.
Kasi: Banaras, the holy city of the Lord Siva.
Kaupinam: Cod piece, Loin cloth.
Kendra: Center.
Khus-khus: The screen woven from vettivera roots. The wet
    screen is used to ward off heat.
Kolam: Design drawn usually with rice  powder.
Korangu: Monkey.
Kripa: Mercy, Grace.
Kripa drishti: Look of mercy or Grace.
Kumkum: Vermilion applied to the forehead, generally by women.

L
Lakshya drishti: One pointedness of the mind; concentrating
    all thoughts on a single object.
Lingam: Symbol of Lord Siva.

M
Maattu Pongal: The day on which cows are decorated
    and venerated in Tamil Nadu. It falls on 14th or 15th
    of January, the day after Pongal (Sankranti).
Mahabharata: Epic story of cousins Kauravas and Pandavas.
    In the battle field Lord Krishna gave spiritual instruction
    to Arjuna known as the Bhagavad Gita.
Mahapuja day: Samadhi anniversary of Bhagavan Ramana’s
    mother.
Maharani: Queen.
Maharshi: Great rishi (seer or sage).
Mahasamadhi: Leaving of the body of a holy person.



Glossary 283

Mahatma: Great Soul.
Mahavakyas: The four main sentences proclaiming the identity
    of the individual soul and the Supreme Being ( Brahman),
    each one taken from each Veda.
Mangai Pillayar kovil: Ganesha temple under the mango tree.
Mantapam: A raised platform of stone with an ornamental
    roof supported pillars.
Mantra: A sacred word or words supposed to have
    Spiritual potency.
Mathrubhuteswara: The god in the form of mother.
Maunam: Silence; a synonym for the thought-free experience
    of the Self.
Mayura: peacock.
Meenakshi: Goddess Parvati whose eyes are in the shape of
    a fish, the consort of Siva.
Moksha: Liberation; spiritual freedom.
Moulvi : Muslim Priest.
Mukti : Liberation from the cycle of births and deaths.
Muni: Ascetic.

N
Naivedya: Food offered to a God at the time of worship.
Na Karmana: From Maha Narayana Upanishad, known as
    Sannyas Mantra.
Nandi: The Vrushabha, the bull, the carrier of Siva.
Nanyar?: Who am I?
Narayana: Lord Vishnu.
Nataraja: Dancing Siva.
Nayana: Father.
Nayanmars: 63 canonised saivaite saints of south India.
    Their stories are told in Periapuranam.
Nirvana: Liberation.



284 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Nirvikalpa Samadhi: the superconscious state in which one
     loses all sense of being different from the universal Self;
    it is a temporary state from which there is a return to ego-
    consciousness.
Nondi : the lame one.

O
Old hall: The present meditation hall where Bhagavan spent
    all his time in Sri Ramanasramam and gave darshan throughout
    the day and night.

P
Palakotthu: Sadhus’ settlement adjoining Sri Ramanasramam.
Palithirtham: The tank next to Sri Ramanasramam.
Panchakshari: Five syllable Mantra.Eg: Namah Sivaya.
Pandal: A large temporary shed erected at the time of function
    or festival.
Pandit: Learned man.
Parayana: Chanting of Vedas or religious or spiritual
    texts  individually or collectively.
Pathala Lingam: Lingam in the pit below the ground level
    of Thousan Pillared Mantapam.
Pavalakunru: The eastern spur of the Arunachala hill on
    which there is a temple.
Payasam: Sweet milk porridge, served as a desert at the end of
    the meal.
Periapuranam: Devotional stories of 63 Saivaite saints of
    South India are told in this work.
Pillaiyar: Ganesha, the elephant headed god who
    removes obstacles.
Pongal: A South Indian dish of rice and green lentil cooked
    with various spices; also the day of Makara Sankranti.
Pooja/Puja: Ritualistic worship performed by Hindus.
Pradakshina: Going round any holy object or place or person.



Glossary 285

Prarabdha Karma: Fate or destiny, the part of ones Karma which
    is to be worked out in the present life.
Prasadam:A sanctified present; food offered to a deity or a
    Guru, a portion of which is returned to the devotee, who
    has offered it.
Pujari: The priest who offers puja.
Punya: Merit; the result of a good deed.
Puranas: Works dealing with Hindu mythology;
Purusha: Man; Supreme Being (who is in the heart of all).
Purushottama: perfect man; the Lord of the Universe.

R
Ramana Dwadasakshari: Twelve syllable Mantra
    on Sri Ramana. OM NAMO BHAGAVATHE
    SRI RAMANAYA.
Rama Navami: Ninth day of the first lunar month of
    Chitra (March – April), Lord Rama’s day.
Rishi Ashram: Abode of a sage.

S
Saameepya: Nearness (to God).
Sadguru\Sat guru: Perfect spiritual master.
Sadhak: A spiritual aspirant; a spiritual seeker.
Sadhana: Spiritual practice.
Sadhu: Ascetic; sage ; sannyasin.
Sahaja samadhi: samadhi which comes naturally and is
    present always.
Sama: Equal; common.
Samabhavatvam: Attitude of equal consideration for
    everyone, and treating all beings alike.
Samadarshan: Equal vision towards all beings.
Samadhi: (i) Absorption in the spirit of self with or
    without trance; (ii) Tomb of a enlightened saint or jeeva.



286 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Samatva: The practice of treating all equally; seeing all alike as
    manifestation of the lord alike as manifestation of the Self.
Samsara: Cycle of births and deaths.
Samudram: Sea or ocean; a large lake in Thiruvannamalai.
Sanchita karma: Karma which has been accumulated in
    former lives.
Sandal: A fragrant paste made by rubbing a piece of
    sandalwood on a rough and moist stone.
Sankalpa: Idea, concept; imagination.
Sannyasi: An ascetic; a person belonging to the fourth stage
    of life.
Sarvadhikari: The chief manager, former administrative head
    of Sri Ramanasramam.
Sarvajna: All knower; Omniscient.
Sastra: Scripture; a science.
Sat: Existence, being.
Sat-chit-ananda: The Absolute which is Being-Consciousness-
    Bliss.
Satsang: Contact with good or pious persons.
Sattva: purity; one of the three primal qualities described as white,
    the principle of purity and goodness.
Self : Atman.
Shariram: Body.
S(h)anti: Peace; tranquillity.
Shashtipurthi: 60th birthday.
Shishya: Disciple, student.
Shrine: Temple.
Shuddha: Pure.
Siddha: One who has acquired supernatural powers and is
    capable of working  miracles; so, a spiritually
    accomplished person.
Siddhi: supernatural powers; realization; attainment.



Glossary 287

Sivanandalahari: A devotional work of Adi Shankara on Siva.
Skanda: Subrhamanya, Kartikeya.
Skandasramam: The abode of Bhagavan Sri Ramana on the
    holy hill Arunachala built by devotee Kandaswamy.
Sloka: Verse or stanza in Sanskrit literature.
Soulabhya: One who is easily accessible.
Sri: Godess Lakshmi; also a short form of Sriman.
Sriman: A honorific prefix used before a man’s name
Srimathi: A honorific prefix used before a woman’s name.
Sushupti: dreamless sleep.
Sveta: white.
Swami: one’s own master; a spiritual guru; used as a
    respectful term for monks.

T
Tapas: Austerities; severe spiritual discipline.
Tapasvi: One who performs tapas.
Thatties: Screen made of grass or thin bamboo strips.
Thousand Pillared Mantap: a big hall with thousand pillars
    in the precincts of Arunachaleshwara temple.
Tirtha: (i) A sacred river or tank; (ii)The sacred water
    or milk abhisheka done to a deity.
Tulasi: A Basil plant, sacred to Lord Vishnu.

U
Upadesa: spiritual instruction given by a Guru.
Uppuma: A South Indian preparation made of semolina or
    broken rice.

V
Vairagya: Dispassion, detachment.
Vaishya: A person belonging to business community.
Vasana: habit of the mind; latent tendency or impression.
Veda: Scriptures.



288 Bhagavan Ramana – The Friend of All Creation

Vedanta Sastra: Sacred books imparting knowledge of the
    Self, like Upanishads, the Metaphysical portion of the Vedas,
    Brahmasutras and the Commentries on them, etc..
Vedic hymns: Mantras in Vedas.
Veena: Stringed musical instrument.
Venba: A Tamil Poetic meter.
Vibhuti: Sacred ashes; God’s glory; supernatural power.
Vichara: Enquiry.
Vichara marga: Path of Self enquiry.
Videha mukta: one who is liberated after death.
Videha mukti: Liberation after death.
Vidya: A spiritual and esoteric discipline, knowledge; learning.
Vimukti: Liberation from samsara.
Virupaksha: One of Lord Siva’s names.
Virupaksha cave: Bhagavan Sri Ramana’s abode of sixteen
    years on Arunachala hill, the cave got its name from
    Virupaksha Deva, a saint from Karnataka who had lived there.
Viss: Measurement to weigh things. 1 viss is equal to 2.2 kilo gram.
Vritti: modification of the mind.

Y
Yoga: Union (with the Supreme Bring).
Yogi: One who follows or has mastered the path of yoga.
